# Validation — Release 1 / Saha v0.2

26 September 2026 (UTC).

## Automated evidence

- Strict TypeScript check passed.
- Production Worker build passed: dynamic app page plus workspace, command, patient history, audit and FHIR API routes.
- **163 checks passed** against the built Worker and local D1. Run metadata and coverage: `tests/latest-result.json`. These are executable HTTP/database assertions, not a claim that all BRD test cases passed.
- Identities: organisation owner, ASHA worker, clinician, supervisor, administrator, outsider and legacy migration fixtures. The owner and clinician intentionally share a display name; one still cannot execute the other's approval.
- Access checks: unauthenticated rejection, no sandbox role switch, organisation and team boundaries, direct ASHA assignments, admin patient/history/FHIR denial, supervisor clinical-write denial, membership suspension and reassignment, including old receipt replay after access removal.
- Membership checks: invitation bound to the recipient account, used/revoked/expired invitations, owner-only changes, last active owner, organisation creation/switching and recovery when the stored active organisation was revoked.
- Consistency: concurrent edits yield one save and one conflict; concurrent identical commands return one committed result; idempotency replay and changed-input rejection; confirmed facts cannot be overwritten.
- Migration: source JSON/revision unchanged; source hash/count ledger; people, visit facts, AI output, receipt, referral, audit and command receipts preserved; earlier unexecuted approval blocked; repeat opening does not duplicate records. An intentionally invalid source forced a transactional failure; no partial organisation/membership/ledger persisted, and restoring the source allowed a successful retry.
- History: original/new identity values, correction author, linked amendments, retained original observations and outputs, wrong-patient blocking, database triggers rejecting mutation, revision/audit pagination without duplicate events.
- Continued workflow: independent simulated submissions, safe uncertain receipt lookup without another send, optional AI permission withdrawal/restoration, output review, synthetic FHIR export and cross-origin write rejection.
- The applied v0.1 migration and snapshot have no source diff. The appended migration is schema-only.

## Browser evidence

Managed preview used synthetic records only. Verified:

1. Existing v0.1 preview data migrated: six people, one confirmed visit and two programme receipts remained available.
2. Organisation/team page, account code, owner/clinical membership and creation of a care team.
3. Demographic correction with required reason; history explicitly showed age 26 before and 27 after, with author and timestamp.
4. Correction draft created from the confirmed visit while the original remained in Previous visits.
5. Two browser sessions opened the same draft. One saved weight 58; the other attempted a stale notes save. The comparison dialog retained the local notes and showed the saved weight. Explicit merge retained both changes, followed by a new save.

The earlier v0.1 browser journey covered the shared visit, confirmation, programme review/approval, partial receipts, recovery and assistance-review flow; those paths were also covered by this release's HTTP regression checks.

Desktop UI inspected. Responsive rules are implemented; a physical-device/mobile-emulation run and 200% zoom accessibility test were not completed. WebMCP remains feature-detected and was not validated in this browser. No hosted staff account was granted access during QA; the Site audience remains owner-private.

## Evidence boundaries

These checks establish the tested synthetic release behaviour, not clinical or production readiness. No live AI/MIS/EHR/ABDM service, FHIR/ABDM profile conformance, clinical validation, external penetration/load test, legal compliance assessment, independent audit attestation or production backup restoration was tested. The live schema is deployed before per-owner first-open migration; fixture results do not by themselves prove a particular owner's live backfill has run.

## Development slice 0a — patient-partitioned storage — 30 September 2026 IST (29 September UTC)

- **1,324 automated assertions passed:** 923 HTTP/D1 (902 retained plus 21 partition checks), 380 contract/checkpoint (361 retained plus 19 storage-contract checks) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports are in `tests/evidence/slice-0a/` with a digest manifest; the R11 result files in `tests/` are retained unchanged as historical evidence.
- The storage contract runs the real migrations in in-memory SQLite through a minimal D1 shim and exercises interleavings HTTP timing cannot guarantee: stale same-patient commits refused with no partial rows, an organisation-level commit invalidating an earlier patient read, an organisation-wide commit refused after a patient commit, and uncertified commands kept organisation-wide.
- A local load probe (`tests/load-envelope.mjs`) measured the R11 model up to 100,006 synthetic people (`tests/load-envelope-r11-baseline.json`) and slice 0a at 1,006 people (`tests/evidence/slice-0a/load-envelope-result.json`). Simultaneous writes on different people went from 3 of 50 accepted to 50 of 50. Throughput at 50 requests/second stayed at 5.9 requests/second because full organisation snapshots remain; that is slice 0b.
- One of two full-suite runs hit the transient local Worker restart recorded in R10 and R11 (a non-JSON 503 at a pre-existing integration toggle step). The rerun passed with unchanged expectations and no request retries.
- TypeScript no-emit and the production build passed. No user-interface change was made, so no browser preview was run for this slice. Hosted D1, Worker limits and multi-account load were not tested; NFR-24 remains unverified and all 176 formal acceptances remain pending.

## Development slice 0b — partition delta responses — 30 September 2026 IST (29 September UTC)

- **1,351 automated assertions passed:** 950 HTTP/D1 (923 from slice 0a plus 27 delta checks), 380 contract/checkpoint and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-0b/`.
- Delta checks run eight certified commands and assert after each that merging the partition delta into the previous full snapshot equals a freshly loaded full snapshot. The first attempt exposed an ordering difference: full loads returned referrals in primary-key order by accident, while the merge appended new rows. Reads now use an explicit order and the merge sorts the same way.
- One run hit the known transient local Worker restart (a 503 on a patient search in the continuity suite, before any slice 0b test). The delta suite's temporary directory was also moved out of the repository to rule out file-watch restarts. The final run passed with unchanged expectations and no retries.
- Browser check with headless Chromium against the built Worker and a synthetic owner: start visit, save notes, reload and see the saved notes, record weight, confirm with the patient check. Every command answered with a delta; the confirmed record displayed correctly; no console errors.
- Load probe at 1,006 people with deltas: one write returns 5 KB instead of 774 KB; 50 simultaneous writes on different people all commit with a median of 1.7 s instead of 3.6 s. Throughput in the mixed sustained phase is still bounded by full workspace reads. TypeScript and production build passed.

## Parallel build integration (slices 3g–6l) — 30 September 2026 IST

- Nine slices were built at the same time by three builders in separate worktrees from `parallel-base` (the configurable test port): lane A 3g, 2c and 3h; lane B 4b, 6i and 6k; lane C 6j, 5a and 6l. Each lane's own sections above record that lane's runs, on that lane's source only.
- Lane A was taken as built. Lane B and lane C slices were merged onto it in order (`int-4b-action-plan` … `int-6l-safe-logs`) with merge commits; no branch was rewritten. Conflicts in the command dispatch and replay chain, test imports, the CI loop, count sentences, the route paragraph in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` and requirement files were resolved by keeping both sides; generated catalogues, traceability and baselines were rebuilt at each step.
- The first full run on the combined source failed five contract suites (action plan, exceptions, report export, audit chain, AI budget): lane B and C harnesses transpile `core.ts`, which imports the slice 2c `fact-provenance` module they had never seen. The integration chain was rebuilt with `fact-provenance` added to those harness file lists at each step; no assertion changed.
- The README slice list was garbled by the automatic merge in the 6j, 5a and 6l integration commits and is corrected in this commit.
- **2,883 automated assertions passed on the combined source:** 1,789 HTTP/D1, 1,073 contract/checkpoint and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-integration-1/`.
- Counts unchanged at 14 implemented-local, 150 partial, 3 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending.

## Slice 6m follow-up — consistent audit chain end in the data export — 30 September 2026 IST (30 September UTC)

- The data export read its tables before verifying the chain, so an export taken while commands ran could hold chained events after the head in its manifest and fail offline verification. The chain is now verified and its head read first, later events are left out, and the manifest records `auditChain.exportedHead`, which `scripts/verify-export.mjs` checks against.
- **3,050 automated assertions passed:** 1,870 HTTP/D1, 1,159 contract/checkpoint (1,157 plus 2 data-exit contract checks: a command committed during the export, and a manifest claiming a later head) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-6m-followup/`.
- First contract run failed the existing size-guard check: its stand-in database had no `first()` for the chain reads that now come first. The stand-in was extended; no assertion changed.
- Requirement statuses unchanged.

## Development slice 0b-3 — paged patient lists and on-demand patient detail — 30 September 2026 IST (30 September UTC)

- **3,833 automated assertions passed:** 2,390 HTTP/D1 (2,296 plus 94 patient-directory checks), 1,422 contract/checkpoint (1,369 plus 53 patient-directory contract checks) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-0b3/`. No migration. Clients that do not ask for paging, including the field notebook and every earlier test, read exactly as before.
- First runs, before fixes: the HTTP smoke expected the sixth seeded person to be in Chronic care; the seeding rule puts that person in Maternal care, so the expectation was corrected (no product change). The contract passed on its first run. The first browser run counted directory rows before the first page had arrived and reported 0; the script now waits for the first row. The My work notice was reworded after that run.

Local machine only; hosted D1, Worker limits, concurrency and reference devices were not tested.

## Next.js security update to 16.3.6 — 1 October 2026 IST

- The supply-chain gate began failing on unchanged code (first seen on the CI run for the hosting pull request): npm advisory 1240609, critical, "Next.js: Remote Code Execution in next/og ImageResponse", affects `next` >=16.2.0 <16.3.6. Saha does not import `next/og` and the gate reported the advisory as not shipped, but the gate fails on any critical advisory, as designed.
- `next` and `eslint-config-next` were raised from 16.3.4 to 16.3.6 with `pnpm install --lockfile-only` (pnpm 11.25.0); no application code changed. The gate then passed with no critical advisory (`tests/evidence/slice-security-next/security-gate.json`).
- Full suite on the updated dependencies: 2,647 HTTP/D1, 1,791 contract/checkpoint and 21 encrypted restore checks passed, the same totals as before the update. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-security-next/`.
- Requirement statuses unchanged (14 / 151 / 2 / 9).

## Development slice 6t — material-read audit, halting when the audit cannot be written, and a chain-head checkpoint — 1 October 2026 UTC

- **4,567 automated assertions passed** on the final tree: 2,699 HTTP/D1 (2,647 plus 52 in the new `tests/access-audit-smoke.mjs`), 1,847 contract/checkpoint (1,812 at the base, which includes the 21 hosted-staging checks, plus 35 in the new `tests/access-audit-contract.mjs`) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-6t/`. `tsc --noEmit` reports no errors. The slice was committed in two steps: the read audit and fault handling first (`f3f8a22`, full suite 2,685 / 1,841 / 21), then the optional chain-head checkpoint and two wording fixes in event details ("1 revision", "1 visit"), after which the full suite was run again with the figures above.
- Fault injection: the contract adds a SQLite trigger that aborts every audit insert for one organisation; the HTTP suite adds the same trigger to the local D1 database for a separate synthetic account's organisation and always drops it. With the trigger, audited reads (history, FHIR download, patient detail) answer 503 with the access message and no content, the audit-chain export and the checkpoint answer 503 with the export message, a command answers 503, an external-request audit stops before the send, and no row of any kind is written; after it is dropped, reads and commands work and the chain verifies intact.
- Checkpoint: the contract rewrites the checkpointed event and every later hash consistently (triggers dropped, as someone with database access could); verification still reports the chain intact and the checkpoint reports `differs`. Over HTTP the checkpoint is the verified head, its digest recomputes offline and issuing it is chained as the next event.
- Measured read cost (contract, in-memory SQLite, a lower bound for D1): one chain-head read and one batch of two statements per audited read, one row written; between 0.20 and 0.36 ms on average over 200 reads across the runs. No receipt is written and neither revision moves, so reads do not make concurrent commands conflict (checked in both suites).
- Harness changes: `tests/ehr-execution.mjs` now writes a stub `access-audit.js` next to its stub `store.js`, because `ehr-execution.ts` imports `access-audit` (document retrieval is not exercised there; its 8 checks are unchanged). `tests/safe-logs-contract.mjs` lists the two new console calls (status or error class only); its 12 checks are unchanged.
- First runs: the new contract passed on its first run, and again on the first run after the checkpoint block was added. The first full run stopped in the new HTTP module, which uploaded media after confirming the visit (refused, so the media id was undefined), and two contract suites failed: `ehr-execution` could not find the new module and `safe-logs-contract` found the two unreviewed console calls; the recovery drill then failed for lack of the HTTP state. The second full run passed every contract suite but the HTTP suite stopped when a later suite could not create an organisation: the new module had created an extra organisation for the shared owner, who reached the ten-organisation limit. The module now uses its own synthetic account. The third full run passed; the fourth (with the checkpoint) passed on its first run. No product code changed between the first three runs.
- Browser checks (headless Chromium, built Worker, synthetic owners), both passed on their first run: after one history read the Activity log showed "View Record History" marked "Record access", and "Show record access (1 loaded)" hid it, kept the other events and showed it again; "Download head checkpoint" saved `saha-audit-checkpoint-1.json`, pasting it into "Saved checkpoint" reported Matches, a changed hash reported Differs, and the Activity log showed the checkpoint event. No axe violations in the log or Audit chain sections and no console errors. `tests/evidence/slice-6t/access-audit-browser.json`, `checkpoint-browser.json`.
- No existing assertion was changed or removed. No route or command added: forty-one method/route handlers and 48 certified patient commands unchanged (`recordAccess` is an internal commit type and is not accepted at `/api/commands`; the checkpoint is a format of the existing `GET /api/audit/chain`). BR-SEC-08 and NFR-10 text and evidence updated and stay partial. Counts unchanged at 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending. This slice has not been independently reviewed.

(2) Run 2: the module then created a new organisation for the assistant part and was refused (422, "up to 10 organisations owned by one account") because the shared owner account already owns ten. Two were product fixes: the audit event linked to a step was the commit's first event about the visit rather than the event about the record itself (now the record, then its visit, then its patient), and a repeated query key (`visit=a&visit=b`) was accepted (now 422). One changed the response: one audit event can link several steps of one commit, so an event's `step` became `steps: [...]`. Two were the test's own expectations (that event's steps also include the demo submission created in the same commit; an exception check used a state whose EHR publication was not yet stale).

No existing assertion was changed or removed.
- Browser check (headless Chromium, built Worker, synthetic owner), passed on its first run: an uncertain demo submission's exception row opened "Trace of this programme submission" showing the linked `executePlan` step with the receipt key and chained audit sequence and no visit note; "Hide trace" closed it; "Open visit", the Receipts tab and the trace disclosure showed the visit's trace with the submission and its audit events; no axe (WCAG 2 A/AA) violations in either trace panel; no page-level horizontal scroll at 390 px; no console errors (`tests/evidence/slice-6y/trace-browser.json`).
- Follow-up in the same slice: a self-review found that the trace's audit lookup bound every item of the visit in one query, which a visit with very many items could push past D1's limit of 100 bound parameters; subjects are now queried in chunks of 40 like the audit ids and receipt keys, and the contract's source check covers all three. A fourth full run on that tree passed with the same totals (3,158 / 2,188 / 21); the evidence reports are from that run.
- One route added (`GET /api/trace`; forty-six method/route handlers). No command and no migration (50 certified patient commands unchanged); correlation references live in the records' existing JSON. ## Development slice 7d — replay and timeout soak of programme and EHR delivery, and adapter overhead benchmark — 1 October 2026 UTC (2 October IST)

- Built from `int7-6y-task-trace` (b0ba193) on branch `slice-7d-replay-soak`. Synthetic data only; no provider or destination was called. Test and evidence only: no product code, route, command or migration changed.
- **5,495 automated assertions passed** on the final tree (third full run): 3,197 HTTP/D1 (unchanged), 2,277 contract/checkpoint (2,270 at the base plus 7 checks in the new `tests/replay-soak-contract.mjs`, bounded to 1,500 counted events) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-7d/`.
- **The first two full runs stopped in HTTP modules this slice does not touch, under machine load** (a 2-CPU machine at a load average of 7 to 13 while four other builders ran their suites). Run 1 stopped after 902 HTTP assertions in `tests/api-smoke.mjs`: of two simultaneous `saveVisit` commands one returned 503 instead of the expected 409 (the 503 does not appear in the application's request log, so it came from the local Worker runtime). Run 2 stopped in `tests/caseload-smoke.mjs`: a transfer scheduled 3 seconds ahead had already become due when the "early apply" request ran (requests were taking up to 6.6 s), so it returned 200 instead of 409. In both runs the restore drill then failed because it needs the HTTP suite's data. Run 1's contract suites all passed (2,277); run 2 was stopped by PID during its contract suites to start run 3. Run 3 passed every suite with no change to the tree.
- **First soak runs (before any full run).** The bounded soak failed on its first run (seed 7) and on four of eight further seeds on its own check that an unresolved EHR publication had exactly one write request: some had none. Cause: the patient read was refused before the write, so the publication was being closed as not sent, but that round's final write was interrupted (an injected fault), leaving the checkpoint running; the later lookup found nothing and left it unresolved. Saha cannot know whether a write started after a lost final write, so leaving it unresolved without resending is correct; the check now allows at most one write and no stored document, and counts these separately (6 in the seed 7 full run). Two harness corrections followed: the event count first included first-time callbacks (now only duplicates count), and replay checks compared a meaningless global counter (now the partner's per-key request count).
- **Soak (NFR-05).** Three full runs (`SAHA_SOAK_EVENTS=10000`, seeds 7, 11 and 23): 10,027, 10,036 and 10,031 counted replay and timeout events, each over about 400 programme and 400 EHR organisations with three items each; no duplicate accepted programme record, no programme submission without a delivered `not_found`, no second stored EHR document or second EHR write, and every final state matching the partner ledger. Seed 7: programme 1,087 accepted, 150 rejected, 11 retryable with all three attempts used; EHR 875 accepted, 106 closed as not sent, 267 unresolved where the EHR stored nothing. Counted in seed 7: 2,117 timeouts and lost responses, 653 same-key and 3,189 fresh-key replays, 2,768 extra concurrent attempts, 826 extra concurrent lookups and 474 duplicate callbacks. Not counted but exercised: 234 HTTP errors, 220 mismatched or changed acknowledgements, 355 interrupted final writes and 9,273 compare-and-swap conflicts.
- **Finding, not a defect:** about a fifth of EHR publications stayed unresolved under these deliberately high fault rates, because the EHR adapter has no definitive not-found answer (EHR-ABDM.md); each needs partner reconciliation and none was resent. Recorded in the slice document as a possible later product decision.
- **Benchmark (NFR-28), local only:** 200 measured iterations per stage on a stated synthetic organisation (200 patients, 600 visits, 300 earlier submissions, 1,500 audit events), partner handler time subtracted, in-memory persistence running the real `rowsForState` diff: p95 129 ms for programme mapping plus send, 167 ms for EHR document preparation plus publication, 124 and 130 ms for the two lookups, on the contended 2-CPU build machine (load average 6 to 9). Not the R1 reference workload or a reference environment; D1 latency is excluded. Report: `tests/evidence/slice-7d/adapter-overhead.json`.
- Harness note: the soak and the benchmark transpile their own module lists (the union of the MIS and EHR execution harnesses; the benchmark also transpiles `store`, `seed`, `partition` and `audit-chain` for `rowsForState`). No existing harness or assertion changed. `replay-soak-contract` added to the CI contract loop; the benchmark is not in CI.
- No route, command or migration (48 method/route handlers and 50 certified patient commands unchanged). NFR-05 current and remaining text and evidence updated, stays partial; NFR-28 updated, stays unverified. Counts unchanged at 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending. Not independently reviewed.

## Development slice 10h — audit of the remaining read paths — 2 October 2026 UTC

- Workspace snapshot loads (`GET /api/workspace`, the snapshot returned with a command, the snapshot of a replayed command), record opens reported by the workspace (`POST /api/record-opened`), patient searches and list pages, the registration duplicate check, indicator results, the JSON operations and care report and aggregate report history each write one chained access event before the read returns and are refused if it cannot be written; every access event also carries the acting membership's role, owner flag, membership version and delegated authority in force (inside the hashed detail). One route added (50 method/route handlers); no command and no migration (`docs/current/SLICE-10H-READ-AUDIT-COMPLETION.md`). Not independently reviewed.
- **Cost measured:** one extra audit row per workspace load: three D1 statements in two round trips (chain-head read, then a batch of the batch-token update and the insert), the same for 6 and for 500 patients in scope (contract test); no receipt, no organisation or patient revision. A viewer-scoped read of the contract's small organisation takes 26 statements. A client that asks for partition deltas (the workspace does) pays nothing extra on commands.
- **6,422 automated assertions passed on the second full run:** 3,706 HTTP/D1 (3,604 plus 102 in the new `tests/read-audit-completion-smoke.mjs`), 2,695 contract/checkpoint (2,643 plus 52 in the new `tests/read-audit-completion-contract.mjs`; `tests/access-audit-contract.mjs` unchanged at 35 and its harness now transpiles `delegation`) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Two existing assertions were changed (below); the number of assertions in each is unchanged. Strict TypeScript passes. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-10h/`.
- **First-run failures, kept as found.** The first full run (14:05 UTC) stopped the HTTP/D1 suite at its 902nd assertion: `tests/database-fixtures.mjs` (`verifyMigration`) counted exactly 3 audit events for the migrated legacy organisation and found 5, because the legacy-migration test loads the workspace twice and each load now writes a `viewWorkspace` event; the count now leaves `viewWorkspace` out. The restore drill failed in the same run (`assert.ok(actor)`: it needs the accounts the HTTP suite creates after that point) and all 2,695 contract assertions passed. Before the second full run, a scratch copy of the HTTP suite that collects failing checks instead of stopping (not part of the evidence) found one more: `tests/audit-chain-smoke.mjs` expected the last chained event after a command to be `saveVisit`; it is now the `viewWorkspace` event of the snapshot returned with the command, so the assertion looks at the last event that is not `viewWorkspace`. The second full run passed every suite. The committed local reporting smoke (`tests/operations-load-result.json`, 20 requests at concurrency 5, each now writing one `viewAggregate` event) had 0 errors with p50 107 ms, p95 140 ms and max 143 ms (88, 127 and 128 ms recorded on 27 September on a differently loaded machine; not a comparison and not a capacity claim).
- Browser check (headless Chromium, built Worker, local D1, synthetic owner): ten checks passed: the first load wrote a `viewWorkspace` event with scope size and the authority clause and the default Activity log page left it out; opening a record from People posted to `POST /api/record-opened` and the record opened after the 200 answer (one `openRecord` event, source patient list), and a repeat within a minute was not recorded again; with the route answering 503 the record stayed closed and the refusal message showed; offline, the record opened from the device with the notice that the open is not in the audit log; the owner's "Include workspace loads, searches and aggregate views" checkbox showed the loads; axe-core found no violations in the Activity log. The first two attempts stopped at a script step that read a response body the page never reads (the application had answered 200); the script now reads the audit log instead and the third attempt passed. Report in `tests/evidence/slice-10h/read-audit-browser.json`.
- Requirement counts unchanged: 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; BR-SEC-08 stays partial (workspace loads, record opens, searches and aggregate views are audited with authority fields; partition-delta responses, the browser's own search over a loaded caseload, offline opens, other patient lists and queues and the pilot, expansion and worker-activity views are not; record opens are reported by the browser). All 176 formal acceptances remain pending.

## Development slice 10c — worker incentive claims — 2 October 2026 IST

- Built from `main` (150b9fc) on branch `slice-10c-incentive-claims`, one of ten slices built at the same time in separate worktrees; not merged. Synthetic data only. The incentive amounts are placeholders in a synthetic unit that the organisation owner sets; no figure is a government or programme rate, no payment is made or implied, and no provider or destination is called.
- **6,452 automated assertions passed on the final tree:** 3,753 HTTP/D1 (3,604 at the base plus 149 in the new `tests/incentive-claims-smoke.mjs`), 2,678 contract/checkpoint (2,643 plus 35 in the new `tests/incentive-claims-contract.mjs`) and 21 encrypted restore checks. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-10c/`. `tsc --noEmit` reports no errors.
- **First runs.** The new contract's first hand runs failed on test errors only (a helper that is asynchronous was used synchronously, a misplaced `await`, visit times outside a period that covers only today, a tamper case that did not alter the first line's patient reference, and an expectation that an administrator who is also an owner could not approve, which the owner rule permits); the product code was corrected only to drop unused helpers. The first full run passed every contract suite (2,678) and the restore drill (21) but the HTTP suite stopped in the new smoke after 902 assertions: the test approved the correction claim with the version it had read before the supervisor verified it (409 "This claim changed"); the test now rereads the claim. The second full run, after that fix, passed with no failing suite: 3,753 HTTP/D1, 2,678 contract/checkpoint and 21 restore.
- Browser check (headless Chromium, built Worker on a fresh local state, a synthetic owner, ASHA and supervisor; `tests/evidence/slice-10c/incentive-browser.json`): 19 of 19 steps passed on the second run. The owner saw the synthetic notice with no schedule and no claims, set a placeholder schedule (version 1); the ASHA previewed two eligible visits (20 SYN), submitted the claim and saw nothing left to claim; the ASHA was offered no verify or approve action; the supervisor verified it, was not offered approval, and received "No approved claim is available to export" before any approval; the owner approved it into chain entry 1 (the panel showed the chain as intact and the 64-character digest), was offered no verify, approve or withdraw action, and downloaded the CSV (digest columns and manifest present, the patient's name and locality absent); the ASHA reversed one line and the new claim 2 was shown as a correction of claim 1 with a -10 SYN line and claim 1 marked as corrected. axe-core (WCAG 2 A/AA) found no violations in the panel for the ASHA, the owner and the ASHA with the correction. The five failed requests are expected: the supervisor's export refusal (404) and four `GET /api/worker-activity` refusals (403) from the existing worker-activity panel for viewers who do not supervise a team, which the panel hides and the browser logs. The first run passed 16 of 18: the script expected the owner to be offered no action on an approved claim, but an owner who supervises the team may prepare a correction (the rule is the claimant or a supervisor of the team), and it counted those four existing 403 responses as console errors; the script now states both expectations. The accessibility audit (`tests/a11y-audit.mjs`) was not rerun for this slice.
- Six commands added (`setIncentiveSchedule`, `prepareIncentiveClaim`, `prepareIncentiveCorrection`, `withdrawIncentiveClaim`, `verifyIncentiveClaim`, `decideIncentiveClaim`; 199 command discriminators) and one route (`GET /api/incentive-claims`, also `?format=csv`; fifty method/route handlers). No patient command (52 certified) and no migration: the schedules and claims are JSON in the organisation `operations` document, bounded by 60 lines per claim, 50 schedule versions and 500,000 bytes. `incentiveExport` joins the access-audit action list (slice 6t). No existing assertion was changed or removed and no existing harness file list changed; the new contract transpiles `incentives`, `incentives-view`, `audit-chain`, `delegation`, `access`, `adapter-operations`, `core`, `fact-provenance`, `mis`, `programmes` and `types`.
- BR-CARE-08 current and remaining text and evidence updated; it stays partial. Counts unchanged at 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending. The slice has not been independently reviewed.

## Development slice 10d — workforce roster import — 2 October 2026 IST

- An organisation owner can upload a roster CSV (`name, account_code, role, team, status`, optional `external_staff_id`), read a dry run (invitations to create, memberships to update or suspend, rows rejected with reasons, conflicts left for review) under a plan hash, download the rejection report, and apply exactly the previewed plan; the apply is refused (409) if anything the plan depends on changed since the preview. Rows are classified by slice 1d's `rosterDiff`, so an import never suspends an owner, never changes the importer's own membership, never reactivates a local suspension and leaves slice 9a fixture clinicians alone. Invitations are ordinary account-bound invitations (72 hours, token hash only). At most 100 rows and 16,000 bytes; each applied row and the batch are audited. New route `POST /api/workforce-import` and command `workforceImportApply` (`docs/current/SLICE-10D-WORKFORCE-IMPORT.md`). No migration: external staff ids are kept in the organisation's `operations` record, capped at 3,000. Built by a builder agent in a parallel lane; not independently reviewed.
- **6,420 automated assertions passed on the first full run of the final source:** 3,688 HTTP/D1 (3,604 plus 84 in the new `tests/workforce-import-smoke.mjs`), 2,711 contract/checkpoint (2,643 plus 68 in the new `tests/workforce-import-contract.mjs`, which is added to the CI contract loop) and 21 encrypted restore checks, 0 contract failures. No existing assertion was changed. Strict TypeScript passed on that run.
- Failures along the way, all before the final run: the first type check failed (TS2322) because the parsed role was typed more widely than slice 1d's roster role type (fixed by typing it as `RosterRow['role']`); three expectations in the first drafts of the HTTP smoke were wrong, not the product (the smoke organisation had no team called "Care team", so the default team name is used; the importer's own row records no staff id, so two ids are recorded, not three; an ASHA's `GET /api/operations` is refused with a 403, so the check reads it as a supervisor, who receives no operations record); and three flaws in the draft contract test were found by reading it before its first run. The final contract suite passed on its first run (66) and again with the staff-id cap test added (68). Two deliberate breaks of the code, removing the provider-account rule and removing the whole-file binding from the plan hash, each made the contract suite fail.
- Browser check (headless Chromium on a built Worker, new synthetic accounts): as the owner, a six-row file gave 1 invitation, 1 update, 1 suspension, 1 unchanged, 1 conflict and 1 rejected row with a plan hash; the rejection report downloaded as a 17-line CSV whose manifest and notice matched, with no names, account codes or staff ids in it; applying created one invitation that the invited account joined with the shown code (200, ASHA), changed the other member's role, suspended the leaver and left the locally suspended member and the owner's own membership unchanged, and recorded the staff id; axe-core found 0 violations after load, after the dry run and after the apply; no console errors and no file contents in any URL or response outside the planned requests. Screenshots and the result file are in `tests/evidence/slice-10d/`. The full accessibility audit (`tests/a11y-audit.mjs`) was not rerun for this slice.
- Limits: formal verification is not done; there is no scheduled or API feed, no upstream de-provisioning, no batch reversal, and no verified or displayed staff id; batches are capped at 100 rows. Slices 1d and 2h are unchanged in behaviour (a dated note was added to each document).
- BR-ID-01 stays partial (its recorded text now names the workforce import and what remains). No requirement status changed: 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending.

## Development slice 10f — household visit scheduling and cross-pack referral consolidation — 2 October 2026 UTC

- Built from GitHub `main` `150b9fc` as lane f of the ten-lane evening build. A household visit plan lists each in-scope member's open programme follow-ups, referrals and routine visit; two organisation commands (`householdVisitSchedule`, `householdVisitCancel`) record or cancel one planned household visit slot that changes no due date, owner or completion condition; and one certified patient command (`createProgrammeReferrals`, 53 in all) records one referral, with every asking programme episode linked as a source and recorded, when episodes of one confirmed visit raise the same need. No route is added (`GET /api/household` gains a `visitPlan` field) and no migration is added. Details and limits: [slice 10f](docs/current/SLICE-10F-HOUSEHOLD-SCHEDULING.md).
- **6,376 automated assertions passed on the final tree, first full run:** 3,693 HTTP/D1 (3,604 before, 89 added by `tests/household-visit-smoke.mjs`), 2,662 contract/checkpoint (2,643 before, 19 added by `tests/household-visit-contract.mjs`) and 21 encrypted restore checks. The HTTP run and every contract suite exited 0 (no `CONTRACT FAIL`). Strict TypeScript (`tsc --noEmit`) and the production build pass. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-10f/`.

Earlier in development, before the full run: the contract test (18 checks then) and the mini HTTP smoke passed on their first runs, after slips in the test text itself had been corrected while writing it (a stray assertion, a 13-request case that the schema rejects by returning no match rather than a 422, a meaningless expression, and a fixture refresh so that the "already cancelled" 409 tests the intended condition). The data-exit check added last (check 19) failed on its first run because earlier checks had replaced the slot list, and was made self-contained.

The check did not cover keyboard-only use, narrow screens, screen readers or the clinician and supervisor views.
- The assertions cover scope (a member outside the worker's scope is counted and never named, with the same refusal as a non-member), no free text in a slot, per-member audit lines that name only that member's items, the unchanged due dates and open items after a slot, one slot per item, cancellation authority, a data-exit check that the existing patient-scope rule leaves out a slot's cover entry for an outside member, one referral for two packs, a third pack joining the open referral, distinct needs and facilities staying separate, and refusals. ## Development slice 10a — scheduled trigger (Cron) foundation — 2 October 2026 IST

- Slice 10a adds a Worker entry (`worker/index.ts`, the Worker's main module) that serves requests through the same Vinext handler as before and adds a `scheduled` handler, a hosted-configuration `triggers.crons` (`*/5 * * * *`, `scheduledCron` in `deploy/cloudflare-staging.json`; empty turns it off), and a scheduled job (`lib/saha/scheduled-job.ts`, `lib/saha/scheduled-review.ts`) that, per organisation within bounded work, applies due caseload transfers through the slice 6ae path, marks provider and destination requests past their time limit as needing review (never retried or resent) and evaluates operational alerts in-app, recording the evaluation. Every write goes through `persist` with audit events naming `Saha scheduled job`. The job is refused in a `misconfigured` environment and calls no external service. No route, command or migration added: 49 method/route handlers and 52 certified patient commands unchanged.
- `tests/scheduled-job-contract.mjs` (32 checks, in the CI contract loop) runs the real modules over the repository migrations in an in-memory database with any outbound `fetch` failing the suite. `tests/cloudflare-staging-contract.mjs` gains 14 checks (25 before; 39 now) on the cron setting; `tests/safe-logs-contract.mjs` now scans the Worker entry and fixes the scheduled log line (13 checks; 12 before).
- First runs: before the first run, a read-through of the new contract's draft corrected expectation errors in the test itself (the order of overdue requests, requests that would have turned late during the test, a maintenance organisation that would have led the queue, an organisation count under the time bound); the first run then failed once, at the statement bound: the queue read itself counted toward the budget, so a bound of 1 visited no organisation. The product was changed so that a run always completes one organisation before the statement and time bounds apply (otherwise a run whose budget is smaller than one organisation never makes progress). The second run failed at the time bound: with the change just described the clock is no longer read before the first organisation, so the test's fake 600 ms step let a second organisation start; the step was changed to 1,000 ms (a test change, not a product change); the third run passed 32 of 32. The first run of the extended Cloudflare contract failed on a misplaced parenthesis in the new lines of the test; the safe-logs extension passed first time.
- Type check clean; the production build exports both handlers (`dist/server/index.js`).
- **6,315 automated assertions passed on the slice's source, full run 1:** 3,604 HTTP/D1 (unchanged from the base), 2,690 contract/checkpoint (2,643 plus 32 for the scheduled job, 14 for the Cloudflare contract and 1 for safe logs) and 21 encrypted restore checks, with no failing suite. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-10a/`.
- **Local served-Worker check (`tests/scheduled-local-check.mjs`, not in the CI loop): 8 of 8 passed** on the built Worker under wrangler 4.92.0 with a fresh local D1: the handler runs on an empty database and logs fixed counters only; a transfer scheduled 20 seconds ahead is not applied early; once due, the job applies it with nobody using Saha and the audit trail names the scheduled transfer actor; alerts are evaluated and the evaluation recorded with an audit event naming the scheduled job; a repeat run records nothing again; with `SAHA_ENVIRONMENT=prod` the job logs its fixed refusal and the database is unchanged. First run: the scheduled call answered 500 `exception`; the cause is that miniflare's static-assets router has no `scheduled` handler, so the check's temporary copy of the configuration drops the assets binding (the deployed Worker keeps it, and its behaviour on Cloudflare is unverified). The second run passed 8 of 8; its result is `tests/evidence/slice-10a/scheduled-local-check.json`, not part of the 6,315.
- Not done: no run on the hosted Worker (no Cloudflare account access here; the trigger is registered by the next deploy), no measurement of detection time, no browser check (the only screen change is one notice line in the assistant panel and one trace step), no independent review. The Workers Paid plan is needed for the job to do its work; the account's plan was not checked.

## CI minutes and a deterministic restore check — 3 October 2026

- The encrypted restore drill in engineering-checks run 36949357077 failed at `tests/recovery-drill.mjs:12` on `assert.ok(!readFileSync(file,'utf8').includes('Meena'))` and the same commit passed on a rerun. The backup file stores ciphertext as base64 text. On 200 draws of 4 MiB of random bytes, the base64 text contained `Meena` 3 times (about 0.5 percent, matching `1 - (1 - 64^-5)^(4/3·N)`); the decoded bytes contained it 0 times, and random salt, IV and tag base64 contained neither `Meena` nor `schemaSha256`. The slice 9a change, which searched the decoded ciphertext bytes and the other envelope fields, still scanned those high-entropy fields, so it was not a proof and was not deterministic. `schemaSha256` was in the same scan.
- The check now parses the envelope, requires the fixed key set, and searches only `format`, `kdf` and `iterations`. It then decrypts with the passphrase and requires `schemaSha256` to match the source inventory and the name `Meena` to be inside the authenticated database bytes. Salt, IV, tag and ciphertext are checked for length and authentication, not for substrings. Locally, after the HTTP suite (3,604 assertions) on a 11,665,408-byte synthetic database that contains `Meena` 242 times: `SAHA_LEAK_TRIALS=40` passed 40/40 in 22 seconds and the old whole-file scan would have failed once; `SAHA_LEAK_TRIALS=80` passed 80/80 in 43 seconds with no whole-file hit; the full drill passed 21 checks three times (about 3 seconds each). The historical `tests/recovery-result.json` was not replaced.
- `engineering-checks` stays pull-request and manual. A new push to the same pull request cancels the in-progress run. A pull request that changes only Markdown or `docs/` does not start the suite (a full run on 2 October was 7m26s, which bills as 8 minutes). Docs-only pull request runs 36953089667 (5m14s) and 36953259450 (7m25s) would not have started. A pull request that also changes code still runs the documentation checks. Start the workflow by hand to check a docs-only change.
- `deploy-staging` push deploys cancel an older push deploy. A hand-started run is a separate queue. While `SAHA_DEPLOY_FREEZE` is `true`, a push skips the deploy job before a runner starts (0 billed minutes). The names were taken from the workflow that has run and from run 36967060269, which logged an empty `SAHA_DEPLOY_FREEZE` and the notice `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN is not set`. The Actions variables and secrets APIs returned 403 for this token, so the settings list itself was not read. GitHub does not allow a secret in a job `if`, so a missing `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` still starts a runner, prints that notice and exits; that short run bills as 1 minute.
- On 2 October, merges #74–#99 (26 pushes, 01:38–01:42 UTC) each started `deploy-staging`. Run 36952218554 (#99) started a runner, reported the missing token, skipped every later step and finished in about 8 seconds. 22 of the 26 completed that way and 4 were cancelled. Those were missing-token runs, not freeze runs. This change does not remove that 1-minute charge, because the secret cannot be read before the runner starts. A later frozen stack of the same size would skip the runner and save those minutes. A real deploy (run 36972654142) took 2m3s; a newer push now cancels an older push deploy instead of letting it finish.
- No requirement status changed: 14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built and 9 unverified; all 176 formal acceptances remain pending.

## Access sign-in fix (slice 0c-1) — 3 October 2026 IST

- The slice 11d identity suite (round 11, testing ecosystem) signed a Cloudflare Access token against a local key endpoint in the real Workers runtime and found that every properly signed token was refused 401. `lib/saha/identity.ts` fetched the Access signing keys with `redirect: 'error'`; workerd throws `TypeError: Invalid redirect value, must be one of "follow" or "manual" ("error" won't be implemented since it does not make sense at the edge…)`, reproduced separately in Miniflare on this date. The error was caught and treated as "no principal", so the hosted site, live behind Access since 2 October, refused every signed-in person. It failed closed.
- Fix: the key fetch uses `redirect: 'manual'`; an unfollowed 3xx is not `ok` and is refused on the next line, so a redirect is still never followed or trusted. `tests/identity-contract.mjs` 18 passed and the type check passed on this branch; the slice 11d identity-hardening suite (28 identity checks) passed with the fix on its own branch.
- Not fixed here: `lib/saha/mis.ts`, `agent-provider.ts`, `ehr-provider.ts` and `server.ts` (`externalCall`) pass the same mode for provider and partner calls, which are off in every hosted environment; they are fixed in the round-11 fix slice with their contract tests.
- Not verified on Cloudflare: this machine cannot reach the hosted site. Sign-in must be confirmed in a browser after the deploy. Requirement counts unchanged.

## Sixth merge into main and the first hosted smoke — 3 October 2026 IST

- At the sponsor's instruction ("Merge 118 and merge 119-125", 11:06 IST), #118 (slice 0c-1, Access sign-in fix) was merged into `main` through GitHub (45f78a8a). `main` was then merged into the top of the round-11 stack (c7a25eb8; both sides already carried the identical identity change), #119–#125 were pointed at `main`, and the stack was merged in one merge commit (f92f54ff) whose tree equals the stack top; GitHub marked all seven merged. Nothing was force-pushed and no branch was deleted. No independent human review took place.
- The deploy of f92f54ff (run 37100461856) built, migrated and deployed the Worker, and the existing sign-in check passed (the deploy of 45f78a8a was cancelled as superseded). The new post-deploy hosted smoke — its first run against the real site — passed 10 checks, skipped 4 (no Access service token) and failed 1: Cloudflare Access answered `GET /api/workspace` with `Origin: https://evil.example` by echoing that origin in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` on its sign-in redirect. The application's Access CORS settings were read in the Cloudflare dashboard the same day: bypass of OPTIONS, allow-credentials, allow-all-origins, methods and headers are all off; the cookie is HTTP-only. The app sets no CORS headers. Without `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`, browsers do not expose a credentialed cross-origin response, and the redirect carries no data, so this is not an exposure; slice 11h changes the check to fail on credentials allowed, a wildcard or CORS on application content, and to record what it sees. The hosted self-test passed 8 of 8 with the change.
- **First full regression on GitHub** (`regression.yml`, started by hand on `main` f92f54ff, run 37100482950): **12,128 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped across 120 suites in 15 min 27 s**, regression guard passed on all 120 — unit 7,193; integration 4,607; resilience 21; smoke 36; UI 17 Playwright tests including the 8 screenshot comparisons (they matched on GitHub's runner); non-functional 191; UAT 63.
- **A flake on GitHub:** the first engineering-checks run of #127 (slice 11h, 4aef25bb) failed the HTTP suite after 10 s; the restore drill then failed because the HTTP suite had not created its accounts. The runner showed only Worker log lines, so the cause is not known. The same step passed locally on the same tree (4,599 and 21) and passed on GitHub on the next commit (9 min 27 s), and the only non-document change since the last green run was the hosted smoke. The runner now prints a failing suite's own error lines first, so the next occurrence shows its cause.
- **A second flake on GitHub, found and fixed:** the next run of #127 (9d9f938b) failed the unit step: the runner's own contract passed 21 of 25 checks on GitHub (four checks that run fixture suites one after another: counting of old-style and report-library results, a failing suite, a suite that checks nothing, the regression guard) while passing 25 of 25 locally. The runner credited a suite with every report file modified at or after its start less 5 ms, so a suite that started within a few milliseconds of the previous suite's report write was also credited with that report; the four failures are consistent with that, although GitHub's log shows only the first line of each assertion. A new check reproduces it on every run by setting an earlier suite's report time into the future: it failed on the old rule. The runner now takes a snapshot of every candidate report file (size, modification time, inode) just before each suite and credits it only with files that are new or changed since. Local results on 3 October 2026 after the fix: runner contract 26 of 26 in five consecutive runs; the unit step 106 suites, 7,194 passed, guard passed; the HTTP suite and restore drill 4,599 and 21; the smoke, security and UAT step 179 passed and 1 skipped — the same counts as before the change in every suite except the runner contract, whose baseline was raised from 25 to 26.
- Sign-in on the hosted site has not been confirmed by a person yet. Requirement counts unchanged (14 / 151 / 2 / 9); formal acceptance execution 0 of 364.

Before edits, install, type check, production build, all 16 migrations in a fresh local D1, 4,599 HTTP/D1 assertions, 7,162 contract/client assertions, 21 encrypted restore assertions and both documentation integrity checks passed. The checkout was materialised from verified current-main blobs; unavailable historical evidence blobs were preserved in the remote base tree rather than invented or deleted.

On the final feature source:

- TypeScript and Worker/client production build passed. The API has **59 HTTP method/route handlers**, with 211 central command discriminators and 56 certified patient commands unchanged. The five image-service handlers use strict schemas; patient workflow writes use the organisation-wide commit guard, while fixed-fixture diagnostics do not write Saha records.
- **4,620 HTTP/D1 assertions passed** (the full 4,617-check run plus three additional diagnostic route checks, reverified in the 21-check affected HTTP module); 21 added for connection readiness, service-off refusal, synthetic example creation/reopen, schema/synthetic confirmation, CSRF and bounded requests. The local Worker restarts four times on the same D1 state to bound dev-runtime heap growth.
- **7,227 contract/client assertions passed** (all 106 earlier suites passed at 7,216; the affected contract was reverified with four additional checks, and the new actual-workerd transport suite passed seven checks) (58 added for image-service scope/consent/environment, real D1 checkpoint/replay, immutable clinical facts, signature/input binding, revoked-consent withholding, lookup-only recovery, synthetic example and corrupt-response withholding).
- The actual-workerd adapter test covers authenticated signed responses, redirect refusal, tampered signatures, pending outcomes, response-size limits and GET-only receipt lookup. Worker fetch uses `redirect:manual` and refuses every non-200/non-202 response. The demo hosting configuration resolves same-zone service URLs through the public front door.
- **21 encrypted local restore assertions passed**, run after the HTTP suite stopped so the source database was quiescent. No hosted recovery or RPO/RTO claim.
- **8 jsdom/canvas component interaction checks passed**: independent ownership card, opening the synthetic fixture, explicit crop approval, server request without browser credentials, result review, quality refusal and connection suspension. Real-browser/camera/mobile visual QA was not run.
- Independent Anaemia Screen's actual built Worker, model and separate local D1 passed **28 HTTP checks** for inference, quality refusal, authentication, strict schema, idempotency, suspension/CAS, restart receipt, quota, body limit and no stored image pixels. Its existing 193 clinical/preprocessing checks, 11 component checks and two browser-WASM parity checks also passed.

The Saha total is **11,868 automated HTTP/contract/restore assertions plus 8 component checks**. Reports and SHA-256 evidence manifest: `tests/evidence/independent-anaemia-provider/`. Historical root reports and baselines remain unchanged. Evidence uses synthetic fixtures and injected transport for Saha's deterministic interleavings; actual provider inference is separately exercised in a Worker.

The actual private hosted Saha → independent hosted Anaemia Screen service passed **8 additional diagnostic checks**, including real model inference and technical quality refusal, with signed results verified by the Saha consumer. This service-credential path uses fixed engineering fixtures and does not acquire a care identity or touch patient records. It is evidence of the hosted service connection, while patient consent/review execution is covered separately by local D1 and jsdom tests.

Limits: no clinical approval, no accuracy claim, no independently validated anatomy detection, no measured-Hb estimation or diagnosis, no clinical capability activation, no production onboarding/billing/SLA, no physical-device or independent security qualification. All 176 formal acceptance cases remain pending. See `docs/current/INDEPENDENT-ANAEMIA-PROVIDER.md`.

The sixth-merge entry above says that answer carried no `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` and so exposed nothing; that was an assumption from the first run's message, which did not report that header, and it was wrong. The anonymous answer comes from Cloudflare Access and carries no application data. Whether another site can read a signed-in person's answer is not tested: it depends on the Access application's cookie SameSite setting and on whether Access adds these headers to the Worker's answers, and the hosted smoke cannot sign in (slice 11g, M5). ## Development slice 12a — voice input on every text and number field — 3 October 2026 IST

- **Request and decision.** The sponsor asked for every field to be voice-to-text enabled as a Saha feature. On-device browser speech (`SpeechRecognition.processLocally`) exists only in desktop Chrome 139+ and is unsupported in Chrome on Android (MDN browser compatibility data, read 3 October 2026), so under the earlier on-device-only rule the microphone could not work on an ASHA worker's phone. The sponsor chose on-device speech where available, otherwise the browser's speech service only when an owner allows it (`setSpeechPolicy`), refused outside the synthetic and training environments. See [slice 12a](docs/current/SLICE-12A-VOICE-INPUT.md).
- **Built.** `lib/saha/speech.ts` (owner setting, recognizer choice, heard text to field values), `app/voice-input.tsx` (a microphone inside the focused text or number box anywhere in the workspace, dialogs included; review before Insert; the owner panel in Organisation & team), `public/field/app.js` (a Speak button beside every notebook text box, review before "Use this"; it replaces the notes-only "Dictate on device" button), 21 message keys in English and draft Hindi. No route or migration is added; one command (`setSpeechPolicy`) is.
- **Defects found while building, fixed before commit:** the review panel opened below a box near the bottom of the screen and its buttons were off screen (now it opens above such a box); the microphone first sat above the box and covered the end of its label (now inside the box, with padding); the microphone's accessible name repeated the box's label, which made label lookups ambiguous (the owner-setup UI journey failed on "Teammate name") and in the notebook the Speak button inside the label changed the box's own name (now the workspace microphone is named "Speak into this box" and the notebook button sits beside the label).
- **Results on 3 October 2026 (shared 2-core container):** type check and build passed; unit layer 109 suites, 7,489 passed (`speech-contract` 19, message catalogue 4,318); HTTP suite 4,648 (28 more, from `speech-smoke`) and restore drill 21; smoke, security and UAT step 179 passed and 1 skipped; UI layer 23 Playwright tests passed, 6 of them voice input on desktop and phone, with the existing screenshot comparisons unchanged and no serious or critical axe finding on the review panel; regression guard passed on each run. The field notebook's ten existing client suites passed unchanged. Baseline: `speech-contract` added; message catalogue 4,108 to 4,318, HTTP suite 4,620 to 4,648, UI 17 to 23. Reports: `tests/evidence/slice-12a/`.
- **Not tested:** no real recognizer ran (a stand-in replaced `SpeechRecognition` in the browser tests); Hindi and English recognition quality, noise and accents on field phones; the browser speech service on a real Android phone; number words. The record does not store which values were dictated.
- Requirement counts unchanged (14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built, 9 unverified); BR-UX-03 and BR-DATA-03 assessment text updated, both still partial. Formal acceptance execution 0 of 364. Built by one agent; no independent review.

- **Medium — the FHIR rule set accepted Provenance under its old version (R21).** `saha-fhir-r4-document-structure` is now 1.1.0 and its basis names the permitted resource types.
- **Medium — R09's programme tag did not reach the workspace scope gate.** `store.ts` `scope` applies `programmeAllows`, so the snapshot and every command that resolves the patient through `core.person()` are narrowed. `tests/purpose-scope-contract.mjs`: 11 checks.

## Development slice 13a — access scope everywhere — 4 October 2026 IST

At the sponsor's request ("fix all", 4 October 2026, 03:10 IST) this slice closes the access findings the integration of #144 left open and adds a server-side backstop for the cross-origin answer of Cloudflare Access on hosted staging ([slice 13a](docs/current/SLICE-13A-ACCESS-SCOPE.md)). On a branch; not merged.

- **Cross-site API requests refused, reads included** (`lib/saha/cross-site.ts`, first in `worker/index.ts`). The first HTTP run failed after a refused cross-site upload: the unread request body turned the next request into a 503 ("Your worker restarted mid-request" in the local runtime), the failure slice 0c had found for `measured()`. The gate now drains the body first; reproduced with the anaemia HTTP suite alone before and after.
- **Facility, locality and programme scope in every check and in the SQL pre-filter** (`bindAccessPolicy`, `withinPolicy`, `policySql`). The new contract compares the SQL predicate with the JavaScript policy on a real SQLite database over 48 policy, grant and member combinations and 45 patients. Deliberately breaking either side fails it.
- **Purpose grants checked at each data-flow checkpoint** (`COMMAND_PURPOSE`, `ensurePurpose`, and the anaemia, frontline, indicator and household routes).
- **Hosted configuration (not code).** On the sponsor's instruction, the agent set the staging Access application's cookie **Same Site Attribute to Lax** in the Cloudflare dashboard (4 October 2026, about 03:20 IST), saved it and reopened the page to confirm. The CORS settings were already all off and were left so; nothing else was changed. A first attempt toggled "Return 401 Response" by a mis-click; that edit was discarded without saving. After the sponsor signed in again (04:01 IST), a script on another site (example.com) in the agent's browser could not read `/api/workspace` from staging, with or without credentials, while the same request from Saha's own page returned 200. This is the deployed code, before this slice. The cookie is `HttpOnly`, so its attribute itself was not read.
- **Local results** (built Worker, Node 22.22.2, Linux container, synthetic data):
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean;
  - unit: 125 suites, 7,996 checks (new: access-scope contract, 8);
  - HTTP/D1: 4,701 (new access-scope smoke: 53), plus the scheduled check, 8;
  - restore drill: 21;
  - smoke: 36 passed, 1 skipped;
  - NFR: 191; UAT steps: 63; UI: 25 Playwright tests.
- Requirement counts unchanged (14 implemented-local, 151 partial, 2 not-built, 9 unverified); BR-ID-02 and BR-DATA-10 texts updated, both still partial. No independent review.

## Development slice 13d — review leftovers — 4 October 2026 IST

Part of the sponsor's "fix all" ([slice 13d](docs/current/SLICE-13D-REVIEW-LEFTOVERS.md)); on a branch stacked on 13c, not merged. Fixes the round-10 review's M2, M3, L1 and L3–L6 and the open findings of reviews 9 and 10 except two.

- **Scheduled job.** The rotation continues from a stored cursor (migration 0016) and wraps; the evaluation and audit-chain records are written by compare-and-set without moving the organisation revision, never in maintenance, and an ordinary operations write keeps them. Breaking the kept-keys rule or the maintenance guard fails the scheduled contract (35 checks).
- **Reads and data flows.** The household view writes one `viewHousehold` event per member shown; an open dispute stops assistant runs; `system` identities are reserved; the operations document is bounded (new contract, 5 checks).
- **Indicators and aggregates.** Refused indicator queries (a combination refusal or a cohort below 5) are recorded with filter names only and limited to 20 per viewer per day; the HTTP check found that the first version recorded only the combination refusal, so the cohort refusal is recorded too. The indicator-releases correction task no longer carries counts or release identities. The aggregate send checkpoint applies the sender's scope before its population screen: in the new contract (4 checks) a report sent while another was between its re-screen and its checkpoint is refused, and removing the fix lets it send.
- **Local results** (built Worker, Node 22.22.2, Linux container, synthetic data): `tsc --noEmit` clean; unit 128 suites, 8,025 checks; HTTP/D1 4,746 (new review-leftovers smoke); scheduled check 8; restore drill 21; smoke 36 passed, 1 skipped; UI 26; NFR 191; UAT 63 steps. The first full run failed four unit and HTTP suites that compared exact text (export schema migration, a source-text check, the reviewed console calls, the smoke's audit detail); each was updated and rerun.
- **Open:** the ABHA review throttle can be raced by parallel requests (needs an atomic reservation); ledger byte use and population-check cost at scale are not measured. No independent review; requirement counts unchanged.

## Development slice 13e — audit log performance — 4 October 2026 IST

Part of the sponsor's "fix all" ([slice 13e](docs/current/SLICE-13E-PERFORMANCE.md)); on a branch stacked on 13d, not merged. Addresses round-11 findings O-4 and O-1; O-2 measured again and still open.

- **O-4 fixed in the local build.** Migration 0017 adds audit indexes (organisation, time) and (organisation, actor, action, time). In Miniflare, back to back on the same build, 1,000 owner workspace loads took 24.9, 26.6, 29.7 and 32.1 s over four rounds without the migration, and 23.8, 23.1, 23.6 and 24.3 s with it. New contract (12 checks) checks each audit query's plan and that the per-load read costs the same at 40,000 events as at 5,000; removing the migration fails it.
- **O-1 mitigated.** Chain retries now wait a random, growing time (at most about 620 ms in all). In a model of 24 concurrent readers on one organisation (real migrations and `persist`, 1–3 ms per database round trip), 50 of 120 reads were refused before the change and none after; new contract, 3 checks.
- **O-2 open.** Worker memory grew 356.5 MB over 4,000 loads without the migration and 380.0 MB with it (KB per load by round 235, 71, 30, 29 against 228, 69, 55, 37): not explained by the audit log, not levelled off, not measured on Cloudflare.
- **Local results on the top of the stack (13a–13e)** (built Worker, Node 22.22.2, Linux container, synthetic data): `tsc --noEmit` clean; unit 130 suites, 8,040 checks; HTTP/D1 4,746 and scheduled check 8; restore drill 21; smoke 36 passed, 1 skipped; UI 26; NFR 191; UAT 63 steps after the documentation was regenerated.
- No independent review of this slice alone; requirement counts unchanged (NFR-10 and NFR-24 texts updated, both still partial).

## Development slice 13f — fixes from the review of 13a–13e — 4 October 2026 IST

Before the "fix all" branches were offered for merge, an agent that had not seen the work reviewed slices 13a–13e (commit 5505e37d against `main`). It reported one high, three medium and three low defects, all fixed here ([slice 13f](docs/current/SLICE-13F-REVIEW-FIXES.md)); on a branch stacked on 13e, not merged.

- **High: the cross-site gate could be bypassed** by percent-encoding the path (`/%61pi/workspace` reached the workspace read cross-site; the reviewer reproduced it). A request another site starts is now refused unless it is a plain page navigation to a non-API path once decoded. The contract covers 13 more spellings; the HTTP suite checks encoded paths over the wire, and checks a real navigation with `node:http`, because `fetch()` always sends its own `Sec-Fetch-Mode`. The first HTTP run failed on that navigation check until it was sent this way.
- **Medium:**
  - A corrected visit counted twice in shadow and drift reports; it now counts once (new check).
  - The indicator refusal limit counted withholds caused by a definition's own minimum cohort, which could lock a manager out. Only differencing refusals count now, CSV exports included (new HTTP check with a minimum cohort of 50).
- **Low:**
  - The rotation cursor now moves only to the processed prefix, and an organisation in maintenance is not queued first (2 new checks).
  - Receivers and assignees must be inside the facility, locality and programme policy (new HTTP check).
  - Safety actions are exempt from the operations bound (new check).
- **Local results on the top of the stack (13a–13f)** (built Worker, Node 22.22.2, Linux container, synthetic data):
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean;
  - unit 130 suites, 8,044 checks; HTTP/D1 4,758; scheduled check 8;
  - restore drill 21; smoke 36 passed, 1 skipped;
  - UI 26; NFR 191; UAT 63 steps after the documentation was regenerated.
- The partial-differencing path has no HTTP test of its own. The fixes were written by the agent that built 13a–13e; no person has reviewed them. Requirement counts are unchanged; the BR-ID-02 text is updated and it stays partial.
